8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap 
Senior Investment Strategist 
Cindy Lam, CFA 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

Expansion at a solid pace  
With economic activity expanding at a solid pace , we expect 
earnings growth to fundamentally drive global equities higher. 
Heading into 2027, rising AI adoption continues to drive 
strong growth  in the technology sector. Recent debates 
about regulating the pace of model developments could 
influence the mix of AI spending, with differing impacts on 
various parts of the AI value chain. However, rising adoption 
and growth in AI use cases should support massive growth in 
capex, benefitting the semiconductor industry, cloud service 
providers and internet platforms. The capex benefits also 
spillover to other industries providing the power infrastructure, 
materials and financing of the investmen ts. The risks are a 
slowdown in AI capex or net job losses from AI disruption, but 
we do not see these having a significant impact yet. 
Rate hikes  from central banks represent a headwind for 
equity valuations, although this has been largely priced in by 
bond markets, given the rise in yields we have seen this year. 
Further rate hikes are being priced by the market. However, 
we expect these headwinds to be offset by solid earnings 
growth in global equities: at 34% in 2026 and 16% in 2027.  
Fig. 21 Global equities continue to be supported by 
strong earnings growth, led by the technology sector  
Earnings growth by sectors for MSCI AC World index

Source: Company reports, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have softened this year  as strong 
earnings growth have outpaced the rise in the market. The 
12m forward P/E of around 20x remains at a premium to other 
markets but justified, in our view, by the high returns on equity, 
differences in sector composition and superior growth outlook. 
In the run-up to US mid-term elections in November, we would 
not be surprised to see higher volatility, though we would use 
any undue corrections as opportunities to add exposure. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ enjoys the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI capex cycle. AxJ also has the lowest 
P/E valuation, given the earnings surge ha s not yet been  
reflected in the market. Within AxJ, we are Overweight 
Taiwan, which benefits from clear visibility on AI -driven 
growth and dominance in manufacturing advanced AI chips. 
We lower China to a Core holding as the valuation re -
rating potential now appears delayed. Share placements for 
AI spending will likely continue, diluting earnings. Regulatory 
concerns still linger from US restrictions on AI chips and 
distillation accusations, even as the Trump-Xi summit signals 
a mutual desire for steady ties. Looming offshore trust taxes 
may lead to further sell down pressures , while domestic 
demand remains weak. However, valuation s remain 
inexpensive with rapid tech innovation and AI -localisation 
investments. Policy stimulus could provide upside. 
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are 
positive on the long-term agreements giving greater earnings 
visibility on memory chip s. Positioning is also cleaner, with 
reduced foreign investor exposure and declin ing leveraged 
ETFs. India remains a Core holding amid positive earnings 
revision and strong domestic demand , alongside  macro 
headwinds from RBI rate hikes. We remain Underweight on 
ASEAN, as oil shocks and a supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 22 Global equity valuations have declined this year, 
with market gains led by earnings growth 
Composition of returns YTD for regional MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Standard Chartered. Min-Max over last 10 years 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal economic growth is supporting solid 
broad-based earnings revisions , with rising rates 
supportive of bank earnings. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is also pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings inflection  after three 
stagnant years, with rising rates supportive of banks’ 
earnings. Fiscal expansion is benefitting domestic demand 
and the industrial sector, although earnings growth in the 
region still lags that of the US and AxJ.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK , 
making it lag the performance of other markets.

Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
Euro area banks: A higher-for-longer rates backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Global Power & Electrification: Rising AI and data centre 
power demand should drive a multi -year grid and 
electrification investment cycle. Power availability and grid 
capacity are emerging bottlenecks to the AI infra buildout. 
Addressing these constraints requires capex and time, which 
supports strong earnings visibility for grid equipment makers. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
MSCI Taiwan: Taiwan’s foundry leadership makes it a critical 
supplier to global tech firms, while its broadening AI hardware 
supply chain is driving strong revenue growth.  
Global High Dividend  payers: Provides a higher -income 
equity allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI 
ACWI and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
US Communication Services: We remain constructive and 
expect further upward earnings revisions, driven by improving 
digital ad-spending and AI monetisation. 
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We upgrade US Materials to Overweight as robust capex 
drives demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials, while we remain bullish on gold . US Technology 
and Communication Services remain the primary growth 
engines, supported by structural AI tailwinds. We remain 
Overweight in Financials across the US, Europe ex -UK, and 
Japan, underpinned by earnings stability  and elevated rates 
supporting interest income. This is further supported by a US 
capital markets recovery  and European balance -sheet 
strength. We remain Overweight in Europe ex-UK Industrials, 
reflecting EU fiscal stimulus and Germany's infrastructure 
acceleration legislation. 
In China, we broaden our Overweight exposure by upgrading 
three sectors: Materials (benefiting from structurally elevated 
commodity prices), Healthcare (biotech innovation and a robust 
pipeline sustaining growth), and Financials (easing margin 
pressure, fee recovery, and capital market reform). Our existing 
Overweight positio ns in Technology and Communication 
Services maintain exposure to China's AI build-out, with fiscal 
deployment into high-tech infrastructure serving as a near-term 
catalyst. On the other hand, we downgrade Utilities to 
Underweight on capped margins and retain our Underweight 
views on Real Estate and Consumer Staples.

Global Market Outlook 18

Our view

• We maintain our Overweight on gold, and our 3 - and 12-month price targets at USD 4,750/oz and 5,000/oz, respectively. 
• We raise our 3-month West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil forecast to USD 100/bbl and 12-month target to USD 80/bbl.

Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 28

Global Market Outlook 28

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Advisory 
Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well -understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manage s to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

## Document 289: EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 19 October 2026 (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IMF ANNUAL MEETINGS
Resilience, but no relief. The IMF's latest global growth forecast is un-
changed from a year ago and from April 2026 (at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4%
in 2027), despite the energy shock, however with a different geogra -
phical breakdown: many energy importers and war-exposed countries
downgraded, and AI-exposed countries upgraded. Resilience was the

buzzword of the meetings, however it appeared mostly in the rear -
view mirror. Looking ahead, buffers are running thin, fiscal policy needs
urgent consolidation, and downside risks (from energy and AI exube -
rance) loom large. Global headline inflation is expected to rise to 4.7%
in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before easing back to 4.1% in
2027 (revised +0.2pp). Growth in advanced economies is revised up to
2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany), staying at 1.8% in 2027. Eu-

rozone growth is upgraded to 1.1% in 2026 (+0.2pp) and 1.2% in 2027.
US growth stays projected at 2.3% in 2026 and 2027 (+0.1pp). Germany
sees the largest G7 upgrade, to 1.3% in 2026 (+0.6pp) and 1.1% in 2027
(+0.1pp). France is the only major advanced economy downgraded, to
0.4% in 2026 (-0.2pp) and 0.7% in 2027 (-0.2pp). For more detailed
takeaways see Substack and this week’s EcoWeek editorial.
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Trade policy, competitiveness and budget agreements by year-end.

EU leaders asked the Commission to propose by year-end an instru -
ment allowing, in extreme cases, full closure of the single market to
a rival deemed to be attacking the EU economy. It is designed as a
“second-strike” deterrent against unfair practices and retaliation.
The Commission is also exploring extending the hybrid-vehicle safe -
guards approach agreed with China last week to other sectors such
as chemicals and machinery. On the "Made in Europe" label, vehicles

would need to be assembled in Europe with at least 70% of compo -
nent value (excluding batteries) sourced locally and with substantial
R&D in the EU. The UK would be included, provided London aligns with
EU trade defence measures. EU Council also called for decisive pro -
gress on the competitiveness agenda, with the single-market (inclu -
ding 26th regime) and simplification files to be wrapped up by year-end.
On the multi-annual financial framework, the objective remains a deal

by end-2026, so that legislation can be adopted in 2027.
EURO AREA
Moderate tightening does it. Multiple ECB Governing Council members,
speaking from the IMF Meetings in Bangkok, re-underwrote their
“moderate response” approach to the ongoing energy-driven infla -
tion surge. Accordingly, they signaled that additional rate action was
likely but not imminent, acknowledging that the rise in bond yields
was doing part of the job, and that there were no signs yet of second

round effects. Strengthening the case for moderation, the Eurozone in-
dustrial production fell in August for the second month running, by
0.5% m/m, pulled down by pharmaceutical output and motor vehicles.
That said, production in Netherlands surged to a new high (2.5% m/m
and 8.7% y/y), driven by AI machinery and equipment.
France
Business starts booming, insolvencies are stable, growth outlook is
downgraded. Business creations rose by 16% y/y in the 3 rd quarter

(+23% y/y in September), driven mainly by the digital sector and retail.
In parallel, business bankruptcies stabilised in the 3rd quarter according
to Altares, with a 0.5% increase y/y (compared with a 5.4% increase y/y
in Q2), and their impact on employment declined: 50,510 jobs at risk
in Q3, down 3% y/y (see our recent analysis). The Banque de France
halved its estimate for Q3 GDP to 0.1% q/q (same as Insee) due to
the manufacturing sector. Its monthly survey showed a sharp jump in

business uncertainty in September, driven by fuel prices and economic
deterioration (the most acute readings in industry and services since
April). The OAT-bund spread was rangebound (137-142).
Germany
Producer prices are rising. Wholesale prices rose 8.7% y/y (vs. 6.8% in
August) due to higher wholesale prices for energy products and inter -
mediate goods, particularly metals.
UNITED KINGDOM
Summer outperformance and kudos from IMF. Monthly GDP defied

expectations in August and posted a 0.1% m/m against a -0.1% consen-
sus. This marked a slowdown from July (0.4% m/m), but also a fourth
consecutive month of expansion. Industrial production underpinned
the advance: it accelerated to 0.8% m/m (consensus: -0.1%, prior: 0.3%)
thanks to pharmaceuticals. The IMF sees growth accelerating to 1.3%
in 2026 (+0.3 pp since July) and 1.2% in 2027. It also singled out the UK
among advanced economies for its efforts and progress in putting its

fiscal house in order.
UNITED STATES
Inflation heats up, manufacturing accelerates, but an October hike
is unlikely. CPI inflation exceeded expectations at 0.65% m/m in Sep -
tember (up 0.2pp), the fastest monthly pace since April. Accordingly,
the y/y rate climbed to 3.7% (up 0.3pp). The biggest upside surprise,
however, came from the core CPI: at 0.33% m/m (consensus: 0.2%, prior:
0.3%) and 2.6% y/y (consensus: 2.5%, prior: 2.4%); as services remained

sticky while goods accelerated to 0.3% m/m on autos and AI-related
items. Producer prices too rose well above expectations to 1.3% m/m
(consensus: 0.6%, prior: 0.5%) – the largest monthly increase since
March 2022 – and 6.3% y/y (consensus: 5.3%, prior: 5.6%). Industrial
production accelerated in September to 0.7% m/m (up 0.6pp) thanks
to utilities. Manufacturing returned to growth (0.3% m/m, up 0.5pp)
with gains broadening beyond the AI-related and aerospace sectors.

Retail sales excl. autos and gasoline, by contrast, slowed sharply to
0.2% m/m (down 0.9pp). During his speech at the IMF Meetings after
the hotter than expected inflation prints, Chair Warsh declined to hint
at an imminent rate hike. We continue to expect the next rate hike to
occur in December.
To watch this week:
- Developed markets Flash PMIs for October (Friday).
- Eurozone Germany September PPI, October ZEW (Tuesday), France Insee Business Climate (Thursday).

- UK August Employment data (Tuesday), September CPI and PPI (Wednesday), September Retail Sales (Friday).
- Japan September CPI, October PMI (Friday).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
JAPAN
Producer price inflation eases. Producer prices fell for a second month
in a row in September, down a further 0.5% m/m, while the yearly rate
slowed for the first time since February (-0.8pp), though it remains
firm at 6.8%. The IMF forecasts growth at 0.7% in both 2026 and 2027,
broadly unchanged. The yen weakened past 158 per dollar again after

reports that the BoJ is leaning against a back-to-back October rate hike.
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Emerging markets
Asia’s outlook upgraded, other regions downgraded.
The IMF upgraded its Asia growth forecast to 4.8% in 2026 and 4.4% in
2027. The largest upgrades are for economies well integrated into the
AI supply chain, notably Taiwan (+5.5pp to 10.7%), South Korea (+0.7pp
to 3.3%), Singapore, Vietnam, Malaysia and to some extent Thailand.
India is forecast to grow 7% in both 2026 and 2027 (fiscal year basis),

up 0.6 and 0.3pp from July on robust domestic demand.
Central Europe’s GDP growth is projected at 2.1% in 2026, slightly
lower than April’s forecast. In 2027, it remains unchanged at 2.3%.
Growth paths are diverse within the region.
In the Middle East & North Africa, GDP is expected to contract by 2%
in 2026. GCC real GDP should fall by 4.8% with only Oman expected
to post positive growth (4.1%). A marked rebound is expected for 2027
(MENA: 7%; GCC: 9.1%).

The growth outlook for Latin America remains subdued, revised down
to 2.3% in 2026 (from 2.4%) and 2.5% in 2027 (from 2.7%). However, in
its "International Trade Outlook 2026" published on 15 October, the
Economic Commission for Latin America and the Caribbean (ECLAC) ex-
pects the region to experience a trade boom in 2026: goods exports are
projected to surge by 20% in value terms (11% from price increases and
9% from volume growth), with trade to Asia (excluding China) soaring

by 32%, while intraregional trade growth lags behind at 8%. Yet, these
export gains are concentrated in commodities and low-value-added
manufacturing, limiting their spillover to broader growth. Stronger
trade is thus providing only a modest boost to the economy.
China
Explosive export growth, subdued inflation and credit growth. In Sep-
tember, export value growth surged to 36.3% y/y from 25% in August,
driven by semiconductors, computer equipment and other AI goods, as

well as green technologies. Export prices surged, especially for semi -
conductors, while total export volumes remained robust. Import value
growth also accelerated to 31% y/y from 28.2% in August, boosted by
both rising volume and rapidly increasing prices. The trade surplus
hit a new monthly record of USD 134 bn in September. CPI inflation
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, mainly because of a 14.9% rise in
vehicle fuel prices. Food prices continued to fall (-1.7% y/y) and core

inflation was stable at 1%. Producer price inflation accelerated to 5%
(the highest since 2022) due to rising commodity prices. Growth in to-
tal domestic credit outstanding (“social financing”) continued to slow
down, reaching a record low of 7.1% y/y in September. Bank loans to
households declined (-2% y/y) for the seventh consecutive month.
In Q3 2026, real GDP growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y (1.2% q/q) from
4.3% y/y (0.9% q/q), primarily led by industrial production (which rose by

5.1% y/y on average in Q3 vs. 4.6% in Q2). On the demand side, exports
were the key driver while fixed-asset investment continued to contract.
Retail sales posted a small pickup in September but remained flat
in real terms in Q3 as a whole. With growth reaching 4.6% y/y year-to-
date, China needs only a 4.2% growth in Q4 to meet the official target of
4.5%-5% for this year – which seems easily feasible. The IMF trimmed
its growth forecast by 0.1pp to 4.5% in 2026 and 4% in 2027.

Indonesia
MSCI launched a consultation on reclassifying the country from Emer-
ging Market to Standalone Market status, with a final decision due by
end-June 2027, citing opaque shareholding structures and suspected
coordinating trading. Since January 2026, MSCI had frozen Index ad -
justments and threatened only a downgrade to Frontier market status
(like Pakistan), but market participants argued that a Frontier reclas -
sification would not properly reflect the extent of the market’s pro -

blems. Over the medium term, the risk is heavy foreign capital outflows
at a time when net FDI no longer covers the current account deficit.
This leaves Indonesia reliant on foreign portfolio inflows and the ru -
piah exposed to a reversal in sentiment. On the day of this announce -
ment, the Jakarta Composite Index fell by 11.6%, its steepest drop since
January 1998. Among Asian emerging markets, Indonesia has posted
the worst stock market performance since the beginning of the year

(-40.7%).
COMMODITIES
Iran/Oil. More of the same. Brent oil prices have remained broadly
stable this week (0.5% at 107$/b on Monday). Renewed Iranian at -
tacks on tankers in the Strait and intensification of Houthis’ attack over
the Saudi territory feed the geopolitical risk premium on barrel prices.
But IEA announcement of an accelerated 100 mb oil release (above
the March initial target of 400 mb) and reports that the US may lift
sanctions on the Russian and Iranian shadow fleets have contributed to

crude price stability. The spread with physical barrel (Dated Brent) has
declined (6.7$/b on Oct 16th). Trump administration’s various attempts
to lower diesel prices have had a limited impact for now. European gas
prices (TTF) recorded another weekly rise (4.7% w/w to 88.4 €/MWh,
the highest level since Dec 22nd, 2022) on the back of the rising number
of attacks on vessels in the Gulf and persisting disruption in the Strait.
However, EU gas inventories reached 74% of full capacities on October

16th (i.e., almost at the 80% month-end target), which should reduce
support for TTF prices going forward.
y/y = year-on-year
m/m = month-on-month
bp = basis point
pp = percentage point
mb = million barrels

## Document 305: First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change of OCR call (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ Consumers Price Index Q3 2026 and change
of OCR call
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 October 22 2026 Share
Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter and 3.7% y/y. Core inflation
pressures are elevated and market pricing is primed for a 25bp OCR hike next week, which
we now expect.
Consumers Price Index, September quarter 2026 – key stats

### Page 2

Headline inflation
Quarterly change: +0.7% (prev: +1.5%)
Westpac forecast: +0.7%
RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

Jio Platforms 上市⾸⽇股价飙升逾 21% ，最新报 1362 卢⽐，远超发⾏价 1119 卢⽐，成为印
度史上最⼤ IPO 。此次募资 3020 亿卢⽐，认购超 22 倍，⻉莱德、 GIC 等顶级机构参与。野
村给予买⼊评级、⽬标价 1340 卢⽐，但实际股价已突破该⽬标，显⽰市场对其⻓期盈利预
期更为乐观。
摘要：
Jio Platforms 上市⾸⽇股价飙升逾 21% ，成为印度史上最⼤ IPO ，全球顶级机构投资者的热烈追
捧印证了市场对这家科技巨头的⾼度信⼼。
股价最新报 1362 卢⽐，较发⾏价 1119 卢⽐⼤幅溢价。这⼀表现超越了机构分析师的普遍预期
—— 野村证券周三给予 Jio Platforms 买⼊评级，⽬标价 1340 卢⽐，较发⾏价隐含 19.7% 的上⾏
空间，但实际股价已⼀举突破该⽬标位。
此次 IPO 共募资 3020 亿卢⽐（约 32 亿美元），认购倍数超过 22 倍，包括⻉莱德、 Capital Group
及新加坡政府投资公司（ GIC ）在内的全球顶级基⾦均参与了锚定投资者配售。这⼀⾥程碑式的

上市，为印度资本市场的⼤型交易注⼊了强⼼剂。
史上最⼤ IPO ，开市 95 分钟售罄
Jio Platforms 此次共发售 2.7 亿股，发⾏价定为每股 1119 卢⽐，融资规模创下印度 IPO 历史纪
录。
据孟买证券交易所（ BSE ）数据，公开认购阶段整体超额认购逾 22 倍，其中合格机构投资者
（ QIB ）部分认购倍数⾼达 70.55 倍，零售投资者部分则认购 2.72 倍，机构需求远超散⼾。
Jio Platforms 董事总经理、印度亿万富翁穆克什 · 安巴尼（ Mukesh Ambani ）之⼦ Akash Ambani
在上市仪式前表⽰， IPO 于 10 ⽉ 21 ⽇开放认购后，仅 95 分钟便完成全额认购。含锚定投资者在
内，此次发⾏合计吸引了 780 亿美元的总需求。
⺟公司领衔， Meta 与⾕歌居前列
据 LSEG 数据，安巴尼旗舰企业信实⼯业（ Reliance Industries ）持有 Jio Platforms 逾 66% 的股
权，为第⼀⼤股东。 Meta Platforms 旗下关联公司 Jaadhu Holdings 持股近 10% ，位居第⼆；⾕

歌国际（ Google International ）持股 7.7% ，列第三。
野村证券周三发布报告，⾸次覆盖 Jio Platforms 并给予买⼊评级，⽬标价 1340 卢⽐。野村在报
告中指出， Jio 的资本开⽀周期基本已接近尾声，预计公司⾃由现⾦流将出现显著改善。

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (October 20)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week . Industrial 
production is expected to edge up from 
5.2% yoy in August to 5.3% in September, 
supported by firm external demand. 
Exports stayed strong, rising 36.3% yoy 
in September, driven by the regional AI -
electronics upcycle.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.3% yoy  in September. 
Household sentiment is weak amid 
uncertain job prospects, slower income 
growth and elevated precautionary 
savings, while subdued property prices 
continue to weigh on household wealth 
and consumption. Investment also stays 
weak, with fixed as set investment 
expected to contract further by 7.5% yoy 
ytd in September. Corporate investment 
is cautious amid the anti -involution 
campaign and geopolitical tensions, 
while the property sector continues to be 
a major drag.

Policymakers are likely to maintain a 
measured policy stance, relying on 
targeted fiscal support to sustain growth 
rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia and Bangko Sentral ng 
Pilipinas (Oct 21 and 22).

Bank Indonesia is expected to extend its 
pause, as they lean on non-rate measures, 
including intervention and hedging 
facilities to help the currency.  On Friday, 
MSCI signaled a  potential shift in 
Indonesia’s status to a standalone 
status, rather than a reclassification to 
frontier market , after consultations are 
complete in mid -2027. Any ensuing risk -
off move in the currency , would likely 
prompt greater central bank vigilance 
and intervention.

Our base case for the benchmark rate to 
be held unchanged this quarter , backed 
by a lower conviction around back -to-
back US rate hikes in Oct and relative 
rupiah stability. Separately, the 
government announced additional 
support for low-income households in 4Q 
by way of in -kind assistance and wage 
subsidies. This year’s fiscal deficit i s 
likely to close in at -2.85% of GDP, close 
to our forecast at -2.9%, but few 
uncertainties facing the 2027 math (note).

We expect a 25bp hike from the BSP to 
5.25% in October , after three rate 
increases earlier in the year. Rising by the 
highest pace in Asia, Philippines inflation 
surged to 7.2% y/y in September from 6.1% 
in August, matching this year's peak and 
approaching a three -year high.  Inflation 
risks remain tilted to the upside, driven by 
further public transport fare adjustments, 
elevated oil prices, recent wage hikes 
and peso depreciation. Fiscal space is, 
meanwhile, limited to support 
households, via subsidies and/ instate 
price caps. Governor Remolona also 
signalled that the BSP remains ready to 
act on rates to contain inflation. With 
inflationary pressures likely to be 
persistent, the likelihood of further hikes 
in 4Q26 rises.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Bank of Korea (October 22)

The Bank of Korea is expected to hold its 
policy rate at 3.00%, following back -to-
back rate hikes in July and August. AI -
driven growth momentum remains strong, 
as reflected in September export growth 
of around 80% YoY. However, the 
urgency for further tightening has eased, 
with CPI inflation stabilising at 2.9% YoY 
in September. The recent KOSPI 
correction and continued KRW strength 
also reduce the need for another near -
term hike. We maintain our call for a final 
25bp rate hike in November, bringing the 
policy rate to 3.25% by year-end.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Japan

September trade and CPI data are due 
shortly. Export growth is expected to 
remain solid at 24% YoY, supported by 
continued strength in AI -related 
semiconductor exports. CPI inflation is 
projected to rise notably to 2.6% YoY, 
driven by the phase -out of publ ic utility 
subsidies, higher global energy prices, 
and persistent inflationary pressures 
from a weak yen. The BOJ is likely to 
increasingly acknowledge that its 2% 
inflation target is within reach. However, 
it is expected to keep rates unchanged 
at 1.25% at its October 29–30 meeting to 
assess the impact of the latest rate hike 
in September.

Taiwan

September export orders and industrial 
production data are due shortly. Export 
orders are expected to sustain 60% YoY 
growth, while industrial production is 
projected to expand by 20% YoY. The 
global AI boom faces growing headwinds 
from hyperscalers’ cash flow pressures, 
central bank rate hikes, and higher bond 
yields. However, our high -frequency 
indicators across AI demand (token 
expenditure), investment (hyperscaler  
capex and data centre construction 
spending), and supply (semiconductor 
sales and exports) show no clear signs of 
a significant slowdown so far.

Malaysia

Malaysia’s external demand likely 
remained strong in September 2026, 
while inflation edged up slightly. We 
expect export growth to sustain its 
robust expansion at 45% yoy , with 
momentum anchored by strong demand 
for electrical and electronics goods. Oil 
& gas shipments likely remained buoyant. 
However, high base effects prevented a 
faster pace of expansion.

We expect headline inflation to rise 
modestly to 2.1% yoy in September 2026. 
The renewed increase in global oil prices 
likely contributed to higher increases in 
domestic energy costs, although fuel 
subsidies continued to limit the cost 
pass-through and keep underlying 
inflationary pressures contained. Food 
inflation likely also firmed, with adverse 
weather conditions associated with El 
Nino posing upside risks.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Singapore

We expect Singapore’s core and 
headline inflation to edge up slightly to 
2.3% yoy and 2.4% yoy , respectively, in 
September 2026, following modest 
increases to 2.2% yoy and 2.3% yoy in 
August. Inflationary pressures likely 
reflected firm private transport price 
increases amid higher petrol prices 
despite slower car price growth , the 
ongoing pass -through of higher global 
energy prices to fuel -exposed services 
items, positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas prices, firmer 
food inflation, and a slight increase in 
still-muted accommodation inflation. 
Firm, albeit not acc elerating, inflation in 
September 2026, which may be close to 
its peak in the Monetary Authority of 
Singapore’s assessment, likely validated 
its decision to maintain SGD NEER policy 
settings at the October 2026 review, 
following two proactive tightening 
moves in April and July.

Hong Kong

CPI is projected to edge up from 1.7% yoy 
in August to 1.9% in September, 
supported by improving consumption 
sentiment and higher global energy 
prices. Mainland visitor arrivals 
increased by 29.0% yoy in September on 
a daily average basis, providing furthe r 
support to retail spending and 
consumer-related services. Meanwhile, 
renewed conflict in the Middle East 
during the month has pushed up global 
energy prices, adding to upward 
pressure on utility and transport costs.

長榮航太 2645 TT  ( 航運業 ) 
長空展翼，航向新高 
投資與評價 
1. 長榮航太 3Q26 受惠維修需求穩健，營收維持季增，惟製造待料、原物料
成本偏高及管理費用增加壓抑獲利，預估 EPS 1.90 元；4Q26 維修需求與
製造出貨動能延續，惟年終獎金及相關人事費用增加，EPS 預估 1.77 元。 
2. 26 年受惠維修工時費率調升、既有機隊延長使用，獲利預估創歷史新高；
27 年維修業務透過人力補充與周轉率改善 ， 製造業務則受惠發動機零組件
需求及空巴 A321 新案投產 ， 帶動營運續創新高 ， 預估26 / 27 年 EPS 8.30 
/ 9.40 元。 
3. 目前長榮航太股價 170.5 元，約為 18.1 倍 FY27(F) PER，位於近三年 15-
35 倍本益比區間中間偏下位置。考量維修業務需求穩定，製造產能維持滿
載。製造業務受惠波音 737 MAX 10 重回民航市場，惟無人機業務仍需等
待，維持買進投資評等，因調整 26、27 年獲利預估，目標價調高至 210
元(22x 27(F)PER)。 
重點更新 
長榮航太 3Q26 維修需求支撐，成本增加但營收維持成長 
3Q26 維修業務受惠棚廠持續接近滿載 ， 新機交付未能完全滿足航空公司需求 ，
帶動既有機隊延長使用年限，支撐定期檢修及結構修復業務。製造業務則受惠
發動機零組件需求增加，但原料到貨時間不穩定，影響生產排程與出貨效率，
營收增加尚未充分轉化為獲利改善。 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元(+1.0% QoQ 、
+17.5% YoY)，優於先前預估 3.9%。惟製造業務原物料成本仍高，下調毛利率
預估至 21.4%(+0.1ppts QoQ) ；因上調管理費用預估，營業費用上調至 2.45
億元(+10.1% QoQ)，營業利益 8.82 億元(-0.9% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 7.11 億
元(-5.6% QoQ)，EPS 1.90 元，營收較去年同期維持成長。 
長榮航太 4Q26 營運受人事成本壓抑，本業營運創歷史同期新高 
4Q26 維修業務受惠既有機隊檢修需求及工時費率調整，棚廠維持滿載。製造
業務隨客戶生產需求增加，維持出貨動能另外，第四季通常認列年終獎金及相
關人事成本，毛利率與營業費用率相對 3Q26 承壓。考量維修人事相關成本提
升，下調4Q26 營收預估 3.4%至 52.26 億元(-0.9% QoQ)，同步下調毛利率至
20.4%(-1.0ppts QoQ)；因預期員工福利費用與研發費用增加，上調營業費用
預估至 2.62 億元(+6.7% QoQ)，但營業利益仍年增 67.2%至 8.05 億元(-8.8% 
QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 6.64 億元(-6.7% QoQ、+39.2% YoY)，EPS 1.77 元，
本業營運創歷史同期新高。

長榮航太 3Q26 維修需求支撐，成本增加但營收維持成長 
長榮航太 3Q26 維修業務受惠棚廠持續接近滿載，新機交付未能完全滿足航空公司需求，帶動既
有機隊延長使用年限，支撐定期檢修及結構修復業務。製造業務則受惠發動機零組件需求增加，
但原料到貨時間不穩定，影響生產排程與出貨效率，營收增加尚未充分轉化為獲利改善。 
3Q26 營收 52.75 億元(+1.0% QoQ、+17.5% YoY)，優於先前預估 3.9%。惟製造業務原物料成
本仍高， 下調毛利率預估至21.4%(+0.1ppts QoQ)；因上調管理費用預估 ，營業費用上調至 2.45
億元(+10.1% QoQ)，營業利益 8.82 億元(-0.9% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 7.11 億元(-5.6% QoQ)，
EPS 1.90 元，營收較去年同期維持成長。

長榮航太 4Q26 營運受人事成本壓抑，本業營運創歷史同期新高 
長榮航太 4Q26 維修業務受惠既有機隊檢修需求及工時費率調整，棚廠維持滿載。製造業務則
隨客戶新機與發動機生產需求增加，維持出貨動能，惟原料到貨時間不穩定，仍影響生產效率與
營收。另外，第四季通常認列年終獎金及相關人事成本，毛利率與營業費用率相對 3Q26 承壓。 
考量維修人事相關成本提升，下調 4Q26 營收預估 3.4%至 52.26 億元(-0.9% QoQ)，同步下調
毛利率至 20.4%(-1.0ppts QoQ)；因預期員工福利費用與研發費用增加，上調營業費用預估至
2.62 億元(+6.7% QoQ)，但營業利益仍年增 67.2%至 8.05 億元(-8.8% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利
6.64 億元(-6.7% QoQ、+39.2% YoY)，EPS 1.77 元，本業營運創歷史同期新高。

維修調價與業外挹注，長榮航太 26 年營運將創高 
展望 26 年，長榮航太製造業務雖隨客戶新機與發動機交付需求增加而成長，但所用原物料受烏俄
戰爭影響，產線效率及改善速度仍在緩步改善。維修業務則受惠先前逐客戶協商調整工時費率，
價格效益持續反映，航空公司延長舊機使用亦支撐維修工作量。現有棚廠接近滿載， 公司將持續
投入技術人員培訓，以利未來增加承接量推升營運。 
長榮航太 26 年營收年增 12.6%至 204.61 億元，受人事與製造成本增加，略下調毛利率預估至
22.5%；營業費用 9.06 億元(+30.3% YoY)，營業利益為 37.06 億元(+23.5% YoY)。稅後淨利預估
3.2%至 31.07 億元(+52.0% YoY)，EPS 8.30 元，營運表現創下歷史新高。

737 MAX 過認證、空巴新案帶動，長榮航太 27 年營運續寫歷史新高 
波音 737 MAX 10 近期已取得 FAA 認證，解除該機型交付的重要限制。該機型訂單逾1,550 架，
約占波音 737 系列待交訂單 31.0%，有助提升後續生產與交付能見度。長榮航太承作其中 LEAP
發動機燃燒段相關產品，應用涵蓋 737 MAX 及空巴 A320neo 系列，研究部認為，後續原廠若順
利提高產量，將有助增加零組件拉貨並改善製造產能利用率。維修業務方面，27 年營運重點轉向
人力補充、第二班安排及維修周轉率改善。現有四座棚廠接近滿載，第五座棚廠須等待桃園機場
土地分配，因此27 年營運仍以既有棚廠效率提升為主。製造業務則除既有發動機零組件外，觀音
二廠預計 2Q27 完工、3Q27 開始投單生產，承接空巴A321 機身件。長異發動機則已恢復現場作
業，將緩步重回過往水準。 
故預估 27 年營收 220.01 億元(+7.5% YoY)。 受惠維修需求穩健 、 製造出貨增加及營運效率改善 ，
預估毛利率 23.4%； 營業費用8.06 億元(-11.0% YoY)， 預估營業利益為43.48 億元(+17.3% YoY)。
稅後淨利 35.20 億元(+13.7% YoY)，EPS 9.40 元，營運續寫歷史新高。

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
個股報告 
2026/10/22

波音 737 製造回溫、無人機業務添翼，維持長榮航太買進評等 
目前長榮航太股價 170.5 元，約為18.1 倍 FY27(F) PER，位於近三年 15-35 倍本益比區間中間偏下
位置。考量波音 737 MAX 10 近期取得 FAA 認證，該機型佔波音 737 系列待交訂單約 31%，逾
1550 架，認證後也降低後續交付不確定性，長榮航太 LEAP 發動機燃燒段相關產品出貨有望增加，
加上空巴新廠亦提供長榮航太中期成長機會。考量 26 年維修業務穩健成長，加上費率調整與業外
改善支撐獲利，預估 EPS 8.30 元；27 年則由既有維修需求、製造出貨增加及費用回落帶動成長，
預估 EPS 9.40 元，營運有望持續走高。 
無人機業務方面，先前 9 月行政院通過《強化國防自主暨無人載具產業發展條例》 ，連續六年編列 
2,400 億元，每年以 400 億元為原則。政策範圍涵蓋可信任供應鏈、研發等。此外，行政院也提出
的 1,457 億元國防追加預算，其中「濱海監偵型」 、 「濱海攻擊型無人機」及「小型自殺無人艇」三
項合計 559 億元 。 長榮航太製造量能充足 ， 加上具備既有監偵型無人載具交付經驗 ， 後續將持續爭
取新案。目前國防部已完成招商說明，希望在預算通過後加快採購，於 2027 年形成初始戰力，目
前仍待國防部公布相關規格。 
考量長榮航太維修業務需求穩定，以及既有製造產能維持滿載。27 年製造業務受惠波音 737 MAX 
10 重回民航市場，無人機業務提供潛在成長機會，維持買進投資評等。惟當前國防部仍未公布無人
機採購規格，仍要等待更多採購細節釋出，本益比由接近平均值之 24 倍下調至中間偏下之 22 倍，
並以 27 年獲利預估，目標價由 195 元(24x 26(F)PER)調高至 210 元(22x 27(F)PER)。

### Page 5

投資 顧問 
個股報告 
2026/10/22

公司簡介 
長榮航太，(Evergreen Aviation Technologies Corporation，簡稱 EGAT）於 1997 年成立，總部
位於桃園市大園區，為長榮集團旗下、長榮航空持股55.05%的航空維修與航太製造公司。主要業務
分為航空器維修及航空零組件製造兩大領域，服務涵蓋機體定期維修、飛機改裝、零組件修護，以
及機體與發動機零組件製造、加工及組裝。2Q26 營收為 52.22 億元，其中維修業務 40.90 億元
(78%)；製造業務 11.32 億元(22%)。 
維修業務方面 ， 公司取得美國聯邦航空總署 （FAA） 、 歐盟航空安全署（EASA） 及日本民航局 （JCAB）
等主管機關的維修認證，除支援長榮航空與立榮航空機隊外，亦承接國際航空公司維修需求。既有
維修機型涵蓋波音 747、767、777 及 787 等，並持續拓展新世代機型服務。依 2026 年 5 月法說
資料，公司已於 1Q26 取得 EASA 的 A350 維修認證，為拓展外部客戶及支援長榮航空預計 2027
年導入 A350 機隊預作準備。 
製造業務方面，公司既有客戶包括波音與GE Aerospace，並於 2025 年與空中巴士簽署合作合約，
成為其一階機體組件供應商，擴大國際民航製造供應鏈布局。此外，公司亦拓展電子產業設備製造
及無人機業務，增加航空維修以外的成長來源。後續營運重點包括新機型維修客戶開拓、空巴新案
導入，以及新廠產能建置與量產進度。 
轉投資方面，長榮航太持有長異發動機維修 49%、長銳航材 49%及長鑽科技 9.3%股權，均於財報
列為採權益法認列的關聯企業。長異為與 GE 合作的發動機及零件維修事業；長銳主要從事航空器
零組件維修；長鑽則提供金屬製品表面化學處理服務，補強公司在發動機、零組件修護及製造製程
的布局。

圖一：2Q26 事業群營收占比

長榮航太 ESG 政策 
完善永續治理架構，強化組織韌性。長榮航太於 2023 年 11 月設立「永續發展委員會」 ，由董事長
擔任召集人、總經理擔任副召集人，並由三位獨立董事擔任委員，將永續發展與風險管理納入公司
治理架構。委員會下設執行小組，由總經理擔任執行長，帶領各部門最高主管，透過 「環境保護組、
社會共融組、公司治理組、產品服務組」四大議題組落實永續策略。2025 年委員會召開 4 次會議、
平均出席率 100%，並向董事會提報 4 次，涵蓋永續工作進度、利害關係人溝通及風險管理執行情
形 。 公司自2024 年將永續報告書編製及驗證程序納入內部控制制度 ， 並於2025 年報告書參考 IFRS 
S2 架構，深化氣候相關風險與機會的財務影響揭露。 
積極減碳與能源轉型，實踐綠色行動。長榮航太依循 TCFD 架構進行氣候風險評估與情境分析，並
於 2025 年重新辨識氣候相關風險與機會，強化財務影響量化評估。公司同年改採GHG Protocol 進
行組織型溫室氣體盤查，涵蓋範疇三並取得第三方確信。節能措施包括更換高效冰水主機、增設空
壓機廢熱回收系統及電梯電能回收，全年節能量達 3,779.34 GJ；包含太陽能建置在內的各項措施，
合計減排 1,030.78 公噸二氧化碳當量。2025 年再生能源消耗量達 5,893.53 GJ，再生能源自發自用
比例達 53.34%，並回收約 84.87 噸廢金屬再利用，持續推動能源轉型與資源循環。 
落實職安與健康管理，打造安全職場。長榮航太以「安全第一，健康優先」為原則，持續運作 ISO 
45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，涵蓋桃園、觀音及松山維修部等所有工作場所，納入 3,257 名員工
及 269 名其他工作者。2025 年共舉辦 113 場職安相關教育訓練，計 2,284 人次參與，並透過危害
辨識、風險評估、事故調查及設備改善，降低現場作業風險。健康管理方面，公司提供優於法規的健
康檢查及全職醫師駐診服務，2025 年共 998 人參與健康追蹤檢查，補助金額約 200 萬元；另推動
特殊作業體檢、異常工作負荷評估、母性健康保護、疫苗接種及肌肉骨骼健康活動，強化員工身心健
康與工作適性管理。 
提升產品品質與供應鏈永續性。長榮航太秉持「安全第一、品質至上」的政策，透過ISO 9001、AS 
9100 及 AS 9110 品質管理系統，確保維修服務與零組件製造符合航空產業要求。2025 年新增 119
家供應商，全數簽署《供應商行為準則》 ，一階供應商簽署回收率達95.37%。公司將 ESG 納入新供
應商遴選評分，權重為 15%，並完成 52 家一階供應商永續性風險問卷調查；另辦理溫室氣體盤查
教育訓練，協助供應鏈建立減碳能力。2025 年客戶滿意度平均為 8.88 分，較前一年下降，公司已
啟動滿意度管理機制，透過原因調查、權責單位改善及後續追蹤，持續精進服務品質。 
培育人才與促進社會共融 。 長榮航太持續投入專業人才培育 ，2025 年員工訓練總時數達 239,565.96
小時，平均每人約 73.55 小時，訓練參與率達 99.46%，並透過專業技術、管理及語言等課程支持員
工職涯發展。同年首次執行員工滿意度、投入度與敬業度調查，其中滿意度及敬業度分別為3.57 分
與 3.86 分，滿分均為 5 分，並依調查結果推動改善措施。產學合作方面，公司與成功大學、逢甲大
學、文化大學、朝陽科技大學及淡江大學合作，舉辦 5 場企業參訪，共 193 人參與。此外，亦透過
庇護工場商品採購、公益贊助、捐血活動、社區互動及藝文支持，擴大企業對人才培育與社會共融的
投入。

## Document 487: インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く ～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実_a6041851

Source PDF: all report pdf/インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く ～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実_a6041851.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 21 日

1/3 
インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く 
～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実現へ難しい対応が続くと予想される～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシア中銀は 10 月 20～21 日の理事会で、政策金利のＢＩレートを４会合連続で
5.75％に据え置いた。MSCI が市場の透明性やガバナンスへの懸念を背景に 「スタンドアロー
ン市場」への格下げ案を示し、株安・ルピア安が進んだことで、一部では利上げ観測が浮上
していた。しかし、中銀は５～６月の累計100bp の利上げで物価安定には十分との認識を示
し、預金ファシリティ金利を 25bp 引き上げて 5.00％とするとともに、為替ヘッジ取引への
優遇措置を強化し、通貨安定と海外資金の流入促進を図る判断を示した。 
○ 一方で、 景気下支えや雇用創出に向けて、 預金準備率を11～12 月に段階的に引き下げ、 銀行
の貸出余力を拡大する方針を示した。原油高に伴うインフレ圧力や、米国の金融引き締めに
よるドル高圧力を警戒する一方、国内消費の底堅さや民間投資の持ち直し、外需の回復など
を踏まえ、2026 年の経済成長率見通しは「＋4.9～5.7％」との従来の見通しを据え置いた。 
○ 中銀を巡ってはここ数年、政策目標に物価・通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用創出を支援
する役割が追加されてきたほか、政治介入に対する懸念もある。こうしたなか、先行きも政
府の景気下支え要請と金融市場の通貨安定要求との「板挟み」となり、様々な政策目標の実
現を目指す観点から、一段と難しい政策運営を迫られる状況が続くと予想される。 
【インドネシア中銀、市場の混乱で一部に政策金利の引き上げ観測も、４会合連続で据え置き】 
インドネシア銀行（中銀）は、10 月 20～21 日に開催した月例理事会において、政策金利である
ＢＩレートを４会合連続で 5.75％に据え置くことを決定した。一方で、預金ファシリティ金利を
25bp 引き上げ、5.00％とした。中銀は、５月の月例理事会でルピア相場の安定を目的に50bp の大
幅利上げを決定したものの、その後も金融市場ではルピア安の動きが収まらず、株式、債券も下落
するトリプル安に直面した。このため、中銀は６月９日に開催した週次理事会で 25bp の緊急利上
げを実施するとともに、ルピア相場の安定に向けた追加対策を発表した。さらに、６月の月例理事
会でも 25bp の追加利上げを実施し、約１ヵ月の間に累計 100bp もの利上げを行うなど、通貨安定
に向けて金融引き締めを強化した。その後は、ルピア相場が落ち着きを取り戻したことから、前回
の９月会合まで３会合連続で政策金利を据え置き、様子見姿勢を維持してきた。 
しかし、 足元では同国の金融市場が再び混乱に直面している。 きっかけは、 指数算出会社のMSCI

### Page 2

2/3 
が 10 月15 日、同国市場を「スタンドアローン市場」に格下げする案を示し、市場参加者との協議
を開始したことにある（注１）。MSCI は
１月、同国株式市場の情報開示に懸念を
表明し、透明性向上への取り組みが進ま
なければ、フロンティア市場に格下げす
る可能性を示唆した。これを受けて、当
局は最低浮動株比率の大幅引き上げな
ど、海外投資家の懸念解消に向けた対策
を打ち出し、市場改革を加速させる姿勢
を示した。しかし、その後も市場参加者
からは、協調的な取引行為への疑念が解
消されておらず、実効性のある法執行も
確認できないとの指摘が MSCI に寄せられた。また、フロンティア市場への格下げでは、問題の深
刻さが適切に反映されないとの意見も寄せられた。このため、MSCI は 2027 年４月末まで市場参加
者の意見を募り、 同年６月末までに最終判断を示すとした。 協議を通じて市場のガバナンスや機能
の実質的な改善を裏付ける明確な証拠が確認されない限り、 格下げを進める方針である。 この発表
を受けて、主要株価指数（ジャカルタ総合指数）は一段と下振れしており、ルピア相場も下落して
いる（図１） 。 
こうしたなか、金融市場においては、月例理事会を前に、中銀が政策金利の引き上げを含む市場
安定策を打ち出すとの見方が浮上した。また、中銀は今回、会合後の記者会見の開始時刻を前倒し
すると発表した。中銀では、７月にペリー前総裁が任期を２年弱残して突然辞任し、上級副総裁で
あったデストリ・ダマヤンティ氏が総裁代行を務めた後、９月２日に総裁へ正式就任した。今回の
月例理事会は、デストリ氏の正式就任後２回目の定例会合に当たる。しかし、中銀は今回もＢＩレ
ートの引き上げを見送る一方、 預金ファシリティ金利の引き上げや為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置の
強化を通じて、市場の安定を図る判断を示した。

【中銀は様々な政策目標の実現に向け、政府と市場の間で「板挟み」となることが予想される】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 今回の決定について 「イラン情勢の緊迫化や米国の金融引き締め
による対外的な圧力が続くなかでのルピア相場の安定」、「2026 年と 2027 年の物価目標の達成」を
目的とする政策ミックスの一環との考えを示した。一方で、 景気下支えと国内の流動性確保に向け
て、 預金準備率の引き下げを決定した。 一般商業銀行向けは現行の９％から、11 月１日に８％、12
月１日に７％へ、 イスラム銀行向けは現行の7.5％から、11 月１日に6.5％、12 月１日に5.5％へ、
それぞれ段階的に引き下げる。さらに、為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置を強化し、海外からの資金流

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
入を促す方針を示した。 
世界経済については「イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の高止まりを受けて、 景気の下押しとイ
ンフレ圧力が併存している」との認識を示す一方、 「2026 年の世界経済成長率は＋３％となる」と
の従来見通しを維持した。 また、 「米国で追加利上げの可能性が高まっているうえ、 財政政策を理由
に米国債金利は高止まりしている」 として、 「新興国への資金流入が抑制され、 ドル高圧力が続く可
能性がある」との見通しを示した。 
一方、 同国経済について 「財政支援や消費者心理の改善を追い風に家計消費は底堅く推移してい
る」としつつ、足元では「企業マインドの改善を背景に民間投資に持ち直しの兆しがでている」と
の認識を示した。先行きは「外需の増加
も見込まれる」として、 「2026 年の経済
成長率は＋4.9～5.7％になる」との従来
見通しを据え置いた。そして、対外収支
も「世界的な不確実性の高まりの影響を
緩和すべく、国際収支を強化する必要が
ある」 としつつ、 「９月末時点の外貨準備
高は輸入の5.3 ヵ月分を有する」とし、
「2026 年の経常赤字はＧＤＰ比▲2.4～
▲1.6％」になると３会合ぶりに見通し
を示した。ルピア相場については「為替
介入やヘッジ取引への優遇措置などを通じた安定強化を図っている」 と従来からの対策を強化する
考えを示した。 そして、 物価動向については 「政府が設定した目標の範囲内で維持されている」 （図
２） ほか、 足元では 「天候不順による食料インフレの懸念が高まっている」ものの、「目標域内に収
めるべく、為替相場の安定に加え、政府も食料インフレ対策を強化する」との見方を示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のデストリ総裁は、MSCI による発表について 「株式市場の動向を
注視している」との見方を示した。そのうえで、政策運営について「的を絞ったものにしたい」と
して、 「海外からの投資の呼び込みに資するものにしたい」との考えを示した。そして、 「我々は５
月以降すでに100bp の利上げを実施しており、 物価安定には十分」 、 「海外投資家に対してインセン
ティブを提供している」との考えを示した。先行きの政策運営については「データ次第になる」と
して明言を避けた。また、ソリキン副総裁は、預金準備率引き下げの狙いについて「雇用創出を目
的としたもの」 、 「銀行の貸し出し余力の拡大を目指したもの」と説明した。そして、今回の政策対
応について「様々な政策目的に対応することを目指したもの」と説明した。 
こうした発言の背景には、ここ数年、中銀に対して、物価と通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用
創出を支援する役割も求められるようになっていることがあると考えられる。 政治介入が懸念され
る動きもみられるなか、中銀の政策運営はこれまで以上に難しくなっている。先行きも、中銀は政
府からの景気下支え要請と、金融市場からの通貨安定要求との「板挟み」に置かれるとともに、難
しい政策運営を迫られる局面が続くと予想される。                  以 上

## Document 640: 宏观点评：经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观点评：经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续.pdf
Report source: 五矿证券
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                             Page 1 / 11

证券研究报告 | 宏观点评 
[Table_Main]  
经济总量边际回升，结构分化延续

[Table_Invest] 
宏观点评  
日期： 2026.10.23

[Table_Author]  分析师  尤春野 
登记编码：S0950523100001 
 ： 13817489814 
 ： youchunye1@wkzq.com.cn  
[Table_DocReport]

报告摘要  
海外宏观： 工业仍有支撑， 就业降温与通胀反弹并存。9 月全球制造业 PMI 升
至 53.0，AI 及设备投资支撑工业，但美国新增非农仅 2.9 万人，三个月 均值
约 5.1 万人， 工资增速放缓。 名义消费仍有韧性， 收入和信心走弱则约束 后续
增长。能源涨价带动美国 CPI 同比升至 3.7%，核心 CPI 也回升至 2.6% 。就
业与物价走势的背离加剧美联储政策两难， 高利率仍是基准环境； 能源冲 击向
核心价格的传导决定后续收紧压力，就业降温则提高继续加息的门槛。  
国内宏观：经济总量边际回升，私人需求修复仍慢。 三季度实际 GDP 同比增
长 4.5%，较二季度提高 0.2 个百分点；名义增速升至 6.0%，为 202 2 年 二季
度以来最高， 有助于居民收入和企业营收改善。 工业、 出口和部分服务消 费提
供支撑，9 月工业增加值、 出口分别增长5.5%、36.3%，高技术制造业和 电子
设备制造业增速快于总体。 但居民消费倾向下降、 制造业产能利用率偏低 ，表
明需求恢复仍不充分。9 月社零同比仅增长 1.1%；我们测算固定资产投 资当
月同比下降 7.2%， 降幅收窄， 但房地产销售、资金和建设链条继续承压。PP I
同比上涨 5.0%主要集中于上游， 终端定价仍弱， 社融增量较多依赖政 府债券 ，
居民信用疲弱。四季度增长持续性取决于居民支出和民间资本开支能 否接续 。 
政策：逆周期调节加力，着力打通资金与需求传导。 财政政策强调增量储备、
加快债券使用和提高资金形成实物工作量的效率， 重点观察地方配套与项 目支
付。10 月起首套购房贷款财政贴息覆盖部分中低总价刚需，有利于降低 购房
负担， 效果仍需结合成交、 合格贷款发放及保交付协同验证。 央行重申不 预设
汇率目标、 保持双向弹性， 并防范急剧贬值风险； 贸易顺差和相对低通胀 支撑
人民币中期渐进升值判断。中美有限降税框架 改善外贸预期，“人工 智能 + ”
政策进一步强调应用和商业转化。 
大类资产： 高利率压制风险偏好， 配置兼顾盈利质量与防御价值。近一个月美
元与美债长端利率上行，科技股和贵金属承压； 10 月 5 日至 16 日，10 年期
美债收益率回落 22 个基点，科技股仍下跌，反映市场更加重视 AI 投入 的收
入与现金流回报。 台积电业绩及资本开支仍强， 产业趋势内应重点验证真 实订
单、应用变现和可持续盈利。同期 10 年期实际利率下降 21 个基点，降 低黄
金持有成本，增长与 AI 盈利不确定性提高分散风险的需求。经过回调震 荡，
若实际利率回落延续， 黄金抗风险价值值得重视； 白银工业属性更强，波 动风
险更高。 国内利率债仍受私人信用偏弱支撑， 久期配置需跟踪财政落地与 债券
供给。 
风险提示：  1、中东冲突再度升级； 
2、中国经济超预期下行。

### Page 2

请仔细阅读本报告末页声明                                                                                  Page 2 / 11

[Table_Page]  宏观点评  
2026 年 10 月 23 日

海外宏观：工业仍有支撑，就业降温与通胀反弹并存 
制造业扩张延续，成本压力同步抬升  
全球制造业继续扩张，但增长与价格的组合发生了变化。 9 月全球制造业 PMI 升至 53.0，较
8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 美国ISM 制造业 PMI 为 54.5， 较上月微降0.1 个百分点，新 订单却
由 53.7 升至 55.3，制造业采购需求仍有支撑。欧元区制造业 PMI 由 52.7 升至 52.9 ，日本
由 54.9 降至 54.1， 均处扩张区间。 与此同时， 美国ISM 制造业价格指数由 71.1 升至 7 7.9，
成本压力明显增强。AI 及相关设备投资、供应链采购支撑工业活动 ，但能源和原材料涨价也
在挤压下游利润，制造业景气回升向居民实际需求的传导仍不充分。  
图表 1：全球制造业保持扩张，工业景气仍有支撑

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
就业和收入放缓，消费韧性受到考验  
美国就业市场降温。 10 月 2 日公布的 9 月非农就业仅增加 2.9 万人， 低于修订后的8 月 13.3
万人，7、8 月合计下修 6 万人；按最新数据，三个月平均新增就业约 5.1 万人。失 业率由
4.1%升至 4.2%，劳动参与率由 61.6%升至 61.8%，失业率上行同时受到劳动力供给 恢复影
响。平均时薪同比增速由约 3.1%降至约 3.0%，企业招聘和工资扩张均趋谨慎。当前 就业总
量仍较稳定，但新增收入动力减弱，会逐步提高居民维持消费增长的难度。  
美国消费尚有韧性，收入和信心却提示后续增长承压。 9 月零售和餐饮销售额名义环比增长
0.8%，剔除汽车和汽油后仅增长 0.2%，能源涨价对总额形成了明显支撑。 9 月 30 日 公布的
8 月实际个人消费支出环比增长 0.6%， 实际可支配收入却基本持平， 支出快于收入的 状态需
要储蓄或融资补充。同期公布的二季度实际 GDP 终值环比折年增长 2.2%，较前次上修 0.7
个百分点，说明此前私人需求底盘仍强；但 10 月密歇根消费者信心初值降至 46.3 ，低于 9
月 48.1。 增长的关键已转向就业与实际收入能否接续支撑消费， 而非仅看名义支出的高增 速。 
能源带动通胀回升， 美联储面临更复杂的取舍 
美国通胀重新抬升，工资放缓尚未抵消能源成本冲击。 9 月 CPI 同比由 3.4%升至 3.7%，环
比上涨 0.6%；汽油价格环比上涨 9.1% ，贡献了总体环比涨幅的一半以上。核心 CP I同比也
由 2.4%升至 2.6%，环比上涨 0.3%，显示价格压力已不只体现在总体指标。 10 月消 费者一
年期通胀预期初值由 4.6%升至 4.7% ，而平均工资增速已低于总体物价涨幅，居民实 际购买
力面临挤压。就业增量放缓与核心价格反弹同时出现，美联储需要在抑制二次传导和 避免需
求过度降温之间权衡，能源能否持续影响核心商品和服务将决定后续政策压力。

欧洲景气修复与能源再通胀并存，日本扩张速度有所放慢。 9 月欧元区综合 PMI 由 52.0 升
至 53.1，服务业由 51.6 升至 53.0，德国改善较为明显；欧元区 HICP 同比则由 3.2% 升至
3.8%，能源同比上涨 18.9%，核心通胀由 2.4%升至 2.5%。总体物价的反弹主要来自能 源，
但需求回升也降低了立即宽松的必要性， 欧洲央行10 月公布的会议纪要继续强调逐次 会议、
依数据决策。日本制造业和服务业 PMI 分别由 54.9、52.5 降至 54.1、51.3，仍保持 扩张，
但速度放缓。 
国内宏观：总量小幅回稳，私人部门需求修复仍慢 
三季度增长回升，收入向消费转化不足  
三季度经济增长小幅回升，内需修复的基础仍需巩固。 三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5%，较
二季度提高 0.2 个百分点， 季调环比由0.9%升至 1.2%，前三季度累计增长 4.6%。第 三产业
当季增长 5.2%， 第二产业增长3.4%，后者较二季度 3.0%改善，但仍低于服务业。工业 、出
口与部分服务消费共同支撑总量回稳，固定资产投资和 房地产开发链条则继续收缩。 值得关
注之处在于名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，是 2022 年二季度以来的最高值， 有助于居民收 入和企
业营收状况的改善。总量增速的回升为四季度稳增长创造了较好起点，持续性取决于 居民支
出和私人资本开支能否跟进。

65.3%。工资性收入和经营净收入分别增长 5.3% 、6.6% ，财产净收入仅增长 1.6% ，收入中
位数增速 4.6%也低于均值。 资产回报偏弱、 不同家庭收入恢复不均， 使一部分新增收 入继续
用于储蓄和资产负债表修复。9 月调查失业率由 5.3%降至 5.2% ，但制造业和非制造业 PMI
就业指数仍低于 50，消费的持续回升还需要就业与财富预期改善。  
生产与外需保持韧性，行业分化继续扩大  
景气调查显示生产恢复快于新增需求。 9 月制造业 PMI 由 49.8 升至 50.1，生产指数由 50.4
升至 51.7，新订单却由 50.6 略降至 50.5，生产与订单的差距 扩大至 1.2 个百分点； 新出口
订单为 50.0。 大型企业PMI 为 50.6，中小企业分别为 49.7 和 48.9 ， 扩张尚未均匀覆 盖企业
群体。非制造业商务活动指数由 49.0 升至 50.2，建筑业与服务业均回到荣枯线上方 ，但新
订单只有 45.7 和 46.7。季节性生产与施工修复已经出现，企业新增订单仍弱，这会 限制补
库存、招聘和投资的持续扩张。 
工业增长较快，产能利用率却暴露出行业需求的错位。 9 月规上工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，
较 8 月加快 0.3 个百分点；高技术制造业、电子设备制造业分别增长 15.3%、16.4% ，仍显
著快于总体，但增速较 8 月有所回落。 三季度工业产能利用率为 73.1%， 较二季度仅提 高0.1
个百分点，同比下降 1.5 个百分点；制造业为 73.3%，较二季度下降 0.2 个百分点。 电子设
备制造业升至 80.2%， 汽车制造业却由70.8%降至 66.2%， 非金属矿物制品业仅 61.2% 。新
产业订单与传统工业闲置产能同时存在，工业增加值较快增长，并未带来制造业产能 利用率
同步回升。 
图表 4：工业生产增长较快

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
外贸继续支撑生产，科技制造的拉动进一步集中。 按美元计价，9 月出口同比增长 36.3%，
高于 8 月 24.9%；进口增长 31.0%，货物贸易顺差约 1335 亿美元。集成电路、自动 数据处
理设备及零部件、电动载人汽车出口金额分别增长约 136%、93%和 102%，与全球 电子及
设备投资需求相呼应。较强出口有助于消化部分工业供给，进口增长则兼有中间品采 购和能
源价格的影响。外需对生产的支撑与国内消费、建筑投资偏弱可以同时持续；后续应 关注海
外真实订单、AI 资本开支和贸易政策落实， 这会影响到 高出口增速能否延续。

图表 5：出口继续高速增长

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
商品消费低位回升，投资与房地产仍承压  
商品零售边际改善，汽车和地产链消费仍是主要拖累。 9 月社零同比增长 1.1%，高于 8 月
0.4%，季调环比由下降 0.16%转为增长 0.35%；剔除汽车后的零售增长 3.9%，餐饮 收入增
长 2.1%。限额以上汽车类零售下降 21.2%，降幅继续扩大，家具和建筑装潢材料分 别下降
3.0%、9.2%， 通讯器材却增长31.1%。 耐用品消费仍呈明显分层，部分科技新品需求 较强，
住房相关消费和汽车需求偏弱。前三季度服务零售增长 5.0%，商品零售增长 1.0% ， 服务消
费为总需求提供了缓冲；但居民整体消费倾向下降，决定了消费修复仍需从品类刺激 转向收
入和预期的持续改善。 
投资单月降幅收窄， 设备更新强于建筑安装。我们测算 9 月固定资产投资当月同比下降 7.2%，
较 8 月下降 10.6%收窄；制造业投资当月下降约 0.9%，好于 8 月下降 6.1%。前三季 度固定
资产投资累计仍下降 7.2%，制造业投资下降 2.1% ，房地产开发投资下降 20.6%，民 间投资
下降 10.4%；国家统计局现行口径的基础设施投资下降 4.2%。设备工器具购置增长 9.7% ，
电子设备制造业投资增长 9.6%，建筑安装工程却下降 10.0%。设备采购和部分新产 业资本
开支已有改善， 但更广泛的项目建设和民营投资仍缺乏订单与回报支撑。 财政资金投放之 后，
需进一步跟踪招标、开工和实际支付，判断投资改善能否持续。  
图表 6：消费低位回升

[Table_Page]  宏观点评  
2026 年 10 月 23 日

新房成交疲弱压低现金回笼，融资不足又限制建设与交付。住建部门统计的前三季度 二手房
网签面积增长 10.7%，显示存量住房交易仍有需求，却难以直接补充开发商回款。房 地产修
复既要改善购房可负担性，也要让合格项目资金覆盖建设期，销售 企稳才能逐步传至 开工与
投资。 
成本上涨与终端偏弱并存，信用依赖财政托底  
价格回升主要集中在生产链上游，终端定价能力改善较慢。 9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，核心
CPI 为 1.0%，服务价格上涨 0.8% ，食品和房租分别下降 1.7%、0.6%；PPI 同比却由 3.8%
升至 5.0%， 采掘、 原材料和加工工业分别上涨25.4%、9.4% 、3.4% ， 生活资料仍下降0.1% 。
能源和资源价格改善上游名义收入，同时抬高中下游成本，消费与住房相关行业的售 价仍受
需求约束。我们测算三季度 GDP 平减指数同比约上涨 1.4%，与二季度约 1.5%接近 ，反映
工业品涨价尚未广泛扩散至全经济价格。后续盈利修复的广度，要看终端订单与售价 能否接
续改善。 
图表 7：通胀水平上升

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
融资总量获得季末与政府债支持，居民信用依旧疲弱。 9 月新增社融 3.55 万亿元，与上年同
月接近； 政府债券净融资1.54 万亿元， 占当月社融约 43%， 同比多增3469 亿元，社 融口径
人民币贷款 1.27 万亿元，同比少增 3347 亿元。金融机构新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿 元，其
中企业中长期贷款 8900 亿元， 住户贷款却几乎没有净增长，上年同月为 3890 亿元。社融存
量同比增速由 7.2%降至 7.1%，M1、M2 分别降至 3.2% 、7.2%。政府融资和企业季 末投放
托住了流量，居民支出与内生扩表需求仍弱；四季度信用改善的关键，是财政资金能 否顺利
进入项目支付、供应商现金流和居民收入，而非债券发行规模本身。  
政策：逆周期调节加力，重点打通资金与需求 
财政与住房政策提供更直接的需求支持  
逆周期政策更加重视增量储备与存量资金使用效率 。9 月 28 日国务院常务会议研究宏观政
策发力提效、促进有效投资，要求“加大宏观政策逆周期调节力度 ”，部署加快债券发行 使用和
重大项目开工，用好地方政府债务结存限额，并研究稳定房地产、促进就业增收的措施。 10
月 9 日财政部发布《2026 年上半年中国财政政策执行情况报告》 ，进一步强调提高政 府债券
资金形成实物工作量的效率、视经济运行谋划增量政策。三季度总量回稳与私人需求 偏弱并
存，政策需要同时兼顾项目建设和居民收入。四季度可重点跟踪结存限额使用、地方 配套资
金和项目支付进度，观察融资支持向实际需求的转化。

2026 年 10 月 23 日

行、 金融监管总局发布 《关于实施居民购房贷款贴息政策的通知》 ， 自10 月 1 日起暂行 一年，
对新发放、 用于购买首套且面积不超过120 平方米、总价不超过 150 万元住房的合格 商业贷
款给予贴息，年化 1 个百分点、最长五年，每户可贴息本金上限 100 万元。政策把财 政支持
由收储和项目端延伸至购房家庭，有利于小城市和城镇房地产 市场的刚需释放，对高 房价城
市及改善性需求的直接覆盖相对有限。后续效果要结合 10 月以后合格贷款发放、新 房成交
和库存变化判断，并与开发融资及保交付安排协同。  
汇率与贸易政策稳定外部预期  
央行重申汇率市场化， 同时更加 关注急剧贬值带来的金融风险。 10 月 8 日， 中国人民银行发
布《中国人民银行关于人民币汇率的政策立场 》，明确不预设汇率目标水平 、不干预长期趋 势 ，
保持弹性与双向浮动；对于短期大幅波动 ，则强调防范“尤其是短期急剧贬值 ”影响金融 稳定 。
我们认为，这一立场为货币政策保持自主性提供空间，也表明稳定预期的重点在于避 免贬值
预期自我强化。较高贸易顺差和相对低通胀为人民币中期渐进升值提供支撑，短期利 差、美
元走势与跨境资金偏好仍会带来双向波动，汇率调整更可能缓慢而有反复。  
中美形成有限降税框架， 外贸政策的可预期性有所改善。 9 月 28 日商务部公布第八轮经贸磋
商相关成果，双方拟就各自约 300 亿美元的自对方进口产品对等降税，并将部分关税 和非关
税措施的暂停安排延至 2027 年 1 月 10 日； 降税在双方完成国内程序后同步实施。 贸 易理事
会、农业工作组等机制为后续磋商提供渠道。相对于双边贸易总量，本轮覆盖规模有 限，短
期作用更偏向降低部分商品的不确定性、稳定订单安排。其对出口的实际拉动仍取决 于最终
产品清单、生效时间与海外需求。 
海外高利率约束未解除， AI 政策更加注重应用 
美联储仍将通胀风险置于政策权衡的核心。 10 月 7 日公布的 9 月议息会议纪要显示，多数
与会者在当时判断年内进一步加息一次可能适宜 ；9 月会议已将目标区间提高至 3. 75%—
4.00%。此后公布的就业和 CPI 分别强化了增长放缓与通胀回升的风险，下一次会议 需要在
新数据基础上重新权衡。我们预计高利率维持仍是基准环境，实际就业降温会提高继 续加息
的门槛，核心通胀若持续加速又会限制宽松空间。  
国内产业政策将应用成效作为 AI 投入的重要牵引 。10 月 16 日国务院常务会议听取“人工智
能+”行动进展，要求“以落地应用牵引技术创新 ”，加强算法、算电协同和高质量数据供 给 ，推
进供需对接与中试验证。政策支持由基础设施延伸至场景和商业转 化，有利于技术向 实体经
济扩散。对投资和企业经营而言，后续应更重视付费订单、单位成本下降和生产率改 善，推
动 AI 投入形成可持续现金流。 
大类资产：高利率压制风险偏好 
近一个月由于美国长端利率再度上升，全球风险资产尤其是非美资产受到较大压制。 按 9 月
17 日至 10 月 16 日统一收盘窗口， 标普500 上涨 0.28%， 纳斯达克下跌1.05%； 沪深30 0、
创业板和恒生科技分别下跌 6.49%、15.95%、4.70%，中国长债指数上涨 1.62%。布 伦特原
油上涨 5.63%，南华工业品下跌 3.14%，伦敦现货黄金和白银分别下跌 4.10%、10. 01%，
美元指数上涨 1.55%。能源供给风险支撑油价，国内需求与盈利预期约束权益，贵金 属则经
历美元、利率和仓位调整的共同压力。

图表 8：主要资产价格近一个月涨幅

资料来源：Wind, 五矿证券研究所 
10 月上旬以来的美债利率回落，主要体现实际利率下降。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，10 年期美债
收益率由 5.31%降至 5.09%，30 年期由 5.66%降至 5.43%，10 年期 TIPS 实际收 益率由
2.95%降至 2.74%；名义与实际利率之差所反映的通胀补偿约维持在 2.35%附近。就 业增量
偏低与信心走弱，使增长预期和防御需求对市场的影响增大。  
科技股定价正在更加重视 AI 投入的收入和现金流回报。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，美债 10 年期收
益率下降 22 个基点，同期纳斯达克下跌 4.87%，创业板下跌 11.58%，说明利率回落 尚未扭
转盈利预期和风险偏好的调整。 产业基本面仍有强势环节：台积电 10 月 15 日披露三 季度美
元营收 467.2 亿美元、同比增长 41.2%，并维持全年 600 亿—640 亿美元资本开支计划 。上
游收入和扩产仍强，市场的担忧更多指向资本开支能否持续、终端付费需求能否覆盖 折旧与
运营成本。权益配置需更加关注企业盈利兑现质量，包括收入、订单、利润率和现金 流等指
标，在产业趋势内提高对盈利兑现的要求。 
经过前期回调与震荡，黄金的配置条件正边际改善。 10 月 5 日至 16 日，金价上涨 1.15%，
而白银继续下跌 6.65%；实际利率回落降低了持有黄金的机会成本，增长与 AI 盈利 不确定
性则提高了组合分散风险的需求。世界黄金协会数据显示， 9 月全球黄金 ETF 仍净 流入约
100 亿美元，配置资金未随价格下跌全面撤 离。我们认为，若实际利率回落能够延续 ，黄金
的抗风险价值值得重新重视。白银兼具更强工业属性和价格弹性，在需求预期走弱时 波动更
大，其组合功能与黄金存在区别。美元再次走强或能源冲击推高利率，仍可能扰动贵 金属的
修复节奏。 
配置上应同时重视现金流质量与防御价值。 国内利率债受益于资金价格回落与私人信用偏弱，
可作为组合防御资产， 久期则需结合财政落地和债券供给调整。 权益重点关注AI 产业 链真实
订单、应用变现和可持续盈利，国内政策受益方向以销售、开工和信用改善为验证。 贵金属
配置更重视黄金分散风险的作用，把握震荡中的配置机会；白 银和原油更适合结合工 业周期
与事件变化进行战术判断。

行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告
发布日后 6 到 12 个月内的相对市场表现， 也即：
以报告发布日后的 6 到12个月内的公司股价 （或
行业指数） 相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的
涨跌幅作为基准。 其中：A 股市场以沪深300指
数为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准； 美国市
场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普500 指数为基准。 
 评级  说明  
股票
评级 
买入 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报在20%及以上； 
增持 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报介于5%～20%之间； 
持有 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报介于-10%～5%之间； 
卖出 预期个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的回报在-10%及以下； 
无评级 预期对于个股未来 6 个月市场表现与基准指数相比无明确观点。 
行业
评级

中性 预期行业整体回报介于基准指数整体水平-10%～10%之间； 
看淡 预期行业整体回报低于基准指数整体水平-10%以下。

1 
PTFE 材料影響性評析

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang)  
新 聞 評 析 
2026.10.15(四)新聞重點摘要： 
NVIDIA 於 2026 年 10 月中旬 OCP 大會上，展示 MGX 伺服器平
台生態系最新進展，更首度公開導入 PTFE( 聚四氟乙烯 )材料的交
換器板 (Switch board) 與相關台廠供應鏈名單。

一. 輝達 OCP 展示 MGX 平台導入 PTFE( 聚四氟乙烯 ) 
NVIDIA 於 OCP Global Summit 2026 上，展示 MGX 伺服器平台
生態系最新進展，更首度公開導入 PTFE( 聚四氟乙烯 )材料的交換
器板 (switch board) 樣品，並欽點 7 家供應鏈合作夥伴，台廠有台
郡(6269) 、台虹 (8039)2 家業者入列，其餘為，中系廠商勝宏、深
南電路、景旺、生益，以及來自美國的迅達 (TTM) 。

二. PTFE 材料特性與 CCL 材料比較，後段加工為關鍵  
宏遠投顧觀察 PTFE(Polytetrafluoroethylene ， 聚四氟乙烯)擁有極
低介電常數、以及極低介電損耗 (在 10 GHz 頻率之下， PTFE Dk
值= 2.1 、Df 值=0.0003) 。此強調極低損耗的聚合物材料，使得
PTFE 性能優於 PPO/PPE 與標準碳氫樹脂 。PTFE 材料擁有出色
的化學穩定性與耐腐蝕性，氟原子均勻分佈於碳主鏈周圍，鍵結
強度是 PTFE 其「穩定性」來源， PTFE 特性抵抗熱劣化、氧化與
化學侵蝕的能力，遠優於碳氫或 PPO 分子，因此，在 AI 伺服器
平台對於高速、高頻傳輸要求之下， PTFE 介電性能具有優勢。然
而， PCB 後段加工會經歷多次壓合的製程， PTFE 材料會面臨諸
多挑戰，由於 PTFE 屬於 Thermoplastic( 熱塑性材料 )，PTFE 材
料較難承受多次層壓 /壓合與 PCBA 製程循環。相對於 PTFE ，銅
箔基板當中常見之， Epoxy( 環氧樹脂，具有高強度黏著、防水防
潮、耐化學腐蝕、優異之電氣特性與絕緣效果 )、PPO/PPE 與碳
氫樹脂屬於 Thermosetting resin( 熱固性樹脂 )， 熱固性樹脂類型產
品，加熱到一定溫度後，皆具備強度高、耐酸鹼、抗腐蝕等優勢，
可於短時間內完成快速且緻密的固化反應，相對適合量產型產線
與加工。

三. 維持台光電 /台燿等高階銅箔基板廠商買進看法  
綜合上述， PTFE 與傳統 CCL 材料特性之比較。即使未來 NVIDIA 
VR(Rubin Ultra) 系列規格設計上，在交換器 CCL 材料供應，可能
會採用 M8 或 M9 與 PTFE 混搭設計，市場盛傳台光電 (2383) 與
相關個股：台光電 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：6,500 元 
     32 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：5,165 元 
     25.2 X 2027EPS 
     2026/10/16 收盤

相關個股：台燿 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：2,050 元 
     31 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：1,615 元 
     24.4 X 2027EPS

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

2 
台虹 (8039)PTFE 混搭 (設計以  PTFE 作為  Core 核心層，搭配  
M9 等級  Prepreg) ，混搭可具有機械強度支撐性，後續加工鑽孔
不易有樹脂殘膠、加工尺寸穩定性也會較容易掌控，宏遠投顧認
為混搭設計會是一個可行性較高的方案。根據市場訊息指出，台
虹進度現已送樣多家板廠驗證，預計 2H27 邁入小量產，初期將
備妥每月 10 萬張 CCL 產能支應；台郡 (6269) 則是鎖定晶片與
PCB 、元件之間的光互連應用，整合軟板、光傳輸介面、光電轉
換 IC，配合客戶開發光電混合傳輸模組應用，宏遠投顧認為後續
可持續關注 2 家台廠確實導入量產之時機 ， 並追蹤後續認證狀況。

2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台光電
(2383) 、台燿 (6274) 為銅箔基板領導廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對於
高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟動，一般
型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級與相關
供應的零組件商機，及攻入 AI 伺服器、高階網路交換器
(800G/1.6T) 的 M8 規格以上 CCL 材料、低軌衛星 CCL 規格轉換
成長可期 。 宏遠投顧 ， 預估2026 年台光電營收為 1,999.92 億元，
+112.17%YoY ，毛利率 34.21% ，稅後淨利 432.76 億元，稅後
EPS+120.77 元。展望 2027 年，在 AI 伺服器需求持續強勁的帶
動下，帶動產能持續增長，預估 2027 年台光電營收為 3,530.14
億元，+76.57%YoY ，毛利率為 35.49% ，稅後淨利 734.03 億元，
稅後 EPS+204.85 元。宏遠投顧，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元， +108.01%YoY ，毛利率 31.13% ，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，
稅後 EPS+38.49 元。隨泰國廠產能釋出，預估台燿 2027 年營收
增至 963.61 億元， +55.73%YoY ，毛利率為 33.26% ，稅後淨利
198.23 億 元，稅 後EPS+66.22 元 。 對於台光電(2383)、台 燿(6274)
銅箔基板廠商，維持買進看法。

## Document 667: 急落した半導体株の健康診断

Source PDF: all report pdf/急落した半導体株の健康診断.pdf
Report source: DLRI

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 16 日(金)

1/5 
急落した半導体株の健康診断 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・先週来、世界的に半導体株が急落している。そこで以下では、一般的に参照される株式の指
標と筆者が定点観測する半導体に関するマクロ指標を点検する。 
・まずＰＥＲ（先行き12ヶ月）を確認すると、日米主要指数は何れも低下基調にある。日経平
均株価のＰＥＲは20倍近傍まで低下し、2025年８月以来の水準にある。S&P500は18倍台まで
低下。これはＦＦ金利誘導目標上限が5.5％という金融引き締め局面であった2023年後半と同
程度の水準である。NASDAQも概ね同様の波形となっている。足もとでは、ＡＩバブルが崩壊
したのか否かに注目が集まっているが、ＰＥＲからみると、バブルを疑うような過剰な株価
上昇は過去数年に一度も発生していない。

・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPYは先行き12ヶ月 155円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12月に 1.5％とした後、27年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FEDはFF金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

・次にＡＩ・半導体株を中心とする企業業績の拡大を裏付けるデータとして、筆者が定点観測

織り込んでいるようにみえる。９月の輸出金額は前年比＋83.7％と好調持続。伸び率は一段
と加速している。また８月の輸出物価統計における半導体価格は同＋175.4％であり、製品価
格は著しい上昇を遂げている。こうした下でメモリ大手２社の収益は飛躍的な拡大を遂げて
いると推察される。中国メーカーが安価なメモリ開発に成功したとの報道が嫌気されたこと
はあるにせよ、輸出金額と株価の方向感の相違が鮮明化している（グラフ内の株価最新値は
10月15日、以下も同じ）。

・台湾の輸出受注額とＳＯＸ指数を比べても同様のことが言える。８月実績は、受注額の約８
割を占めるＩＴ関連財は電子部品が前年比＋83.9％、情報通信技術（ＩＣＴ）製品が同＋
100.5％であり、全体として同＋71.4％と拡大基調にある。一方で世界の代表的な半導体銘柄
で構成されるＳＯＸ指数は大幅に調整しており、その輸出受注との乖離度合いは、2025年春
にトランプ関税と対中半導体輸出規制の強化が同時に意識された局面よりも際立っている。

## Document 734: 米インフレの動向－関税・エネルギー・AI投資による物価上昇を検証

Source PDF: all report pdf/米インフレの動向－関税・エネルギー・AI投資による物価上昇を検証.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

（図表１）

消費者物価主要指数
CPI総合 CPIコア
（％）
（注）前年同月比。
（資料）労働統計局（BLS）よりニッセイ基礎研究所作成
１. 米国の26年 9月の消費者物価指数（ＣＰＩ）は、総合指数が前年同月比+3.7％（前月：
+3.4％）と再加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が押し上げたほか、コア指数（食料品・エネ
ルギーを除く）も同+2.6％（前月：+2.4％）と小幅に加速。一方、コア財価格の伸びは
限定的。 
２. 関税は25年春以降のコア財価格を押し上げたものの、実効関税率の低下などから影響は
当初想定を下回った。今後は価格転嫁の一巡や前年のベース効果の剥落に伴い、既存関
税による押し上げ効果は減衰する見込み。ただし、追加関税による物価上昇リスクは残
る。 
３. 一方、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油・ガソリン価格の上昇が、足元のインフレ再加速の
主因となっている。エネルギー価格の上昇は総合ＣＰＩを直接押し上げるほか、輸送費
や生産コストを通じて他の財・サービス価格に波及する可能性がある。 
４. また、ＡＩ関連投資に伴う電力需要拡大も、電力料金の上昇要因として注目される。電
力料金は26年9月に前年同月比+4.4％（前月：+3.8％）と加速。もっとも、燃料費や送
配電投資なども影響するため、ＡＩ関連投資による押し上げ効果は慎重な評価が必要。 
５. 当研究所では、ＣＰＩ上昇率を26年が前年比+3.4％、27年が同+2.5％と予想。26年は
エネルギー価格の上昇などから前年の+2.6％を上回る一方、27年は既存関税の押し上げ
効果の減衰などから鈍化する見込み。ただし、エネルギー価格や追加関税、電力料金な
どが上振れリスクとなろう。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-23 
米インフレの動向 
－関税・エネルギー・AI投資による物価上昇を検証

経済研究部 主任研究員 窪谷 浩

### Page 2

２． 足元のインフレ動向と物価押し上げ要因 
（ＣＰＩの動向）エネルギー価格の上昇で総合指数が再加速 
米国のＣＰＩは、25年春以降に関税引き上げの影響などから上昇した後、いったん低下したもの
の、26年春先以降は再び加速している。総合指数（前年同月比）は25年 4月の+2.3％から同年 9
月に+3.0％まで上昇した後、26年2月には+2.4％まで低下した。しかし、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う
エネルギー価格の上昇を背景に、3月には+3.3％と再加速し、9月には+3.7％まで上昇した（前掲図
表 1） 。一方、コア指数（食料品・エネルギーを除く）は25年 7～8月の+3.1％から 26年 8月には
+2.4％まで低下した後、9月は+2.6％と小幅に加速したものの、 総合指数との乖離は拡大している。 
ＣＰＩの内訳をみると、 エネルギー価格は26
年 2 月の前年同月比 +0.5％から 3 月には
+12.5％へ急伸し、9月には+20.4％まで上昇す
るなど、総合指数の再加速を主導している（図
表2） 。一方、コア財価格は25年春以降、関税
引き上げの影響などからプラスに転じ、同年8
～9月には+1.5％まで上昇したものの、26年 9
月には+0.7％まで鈍化した。また、コアサービ
ス価格のうち、住居費の伸びは 23年以降大幅
に鈍化したものの、26年春先に再び加速し、足
元でも+3％台前半で下げ渋っている。一方、住
居費を除くサービスも春先に伸びが加速した後、足元では+3％程度で推移している。 
このように、足元のインフレ再加速は主にエネルギー価格の上昇によるものであり、コア財価格
の伸びは鈍化しているほか、サービス価格の上昇圧力はピーク時から緩和したものの、足元では根
強さがみられる。

### Page 3

こうした中、ニューヨーク連銀の試算では、関税による消費財価格上昇率への寄与度は 26年後
半にマイナスに転じ、27年１月には▲0.7％ポイントとなる見通しである（前掲図表３） 。これは、
25年の大幅な関税引き上げによる価格上昇が前年比の比較対象から外れるためである。もっとも、
関税による物価水準の押し上げ効果は、関税率の引き下げなどから縮小するものの、消失するわけ
ではない。 
このように、既存関税によるインフレ押し上げ効果は今後減衰すると見込まれる。ただし、トラ
ンプ政権は通商法301条に基づく追加関税措置を進めており、今後の通商政策次第では、インフレ
率が再び押し上げられる可能性がある。

（エネルギー価格の影響）中東情勢の緊迫化がインフレを押し上げ 
エネルギー価格は、26年 2月末に始まったイラン戦争を背景に大幅に上昇している。原油先物価
格（ＷＴＩ）は25年末にかけて1バレル60ドル前後まで低下していたが、イラン戦争の開始を受
けて急騰し、26年春先には一時110ドルを上回った（図表5） 。その後、いったん70ドル前後まで
下落したものの、足元では再び上昇しており、10月 22日には 99.5ドルと高水準で推移している。

（注）関税による非石油系消費財価格の前年比上昇率への寄与度。直接効果は関税対象品目への
価格転嫁、競合価格効果は競合企業の価格調整、コスト上昇効果は輸入中間財などの費用増加を

（資料）ニューヨーク連銀「Liberty Street Economics」（2026年10月6日）よりニッセイ基礎研究所作成

### Page 4

（ＡＩ関連投資の影響）電力需要の拡大が電力料金の上昇圧力に 
ＡＩ関連投資の拡大に伴うデータセンターの電力需要増加も、電力料金の上昇要因として注目さ
れている。米国の電力料金（消費者物価指数、前年同月比）は、22年 8月に+15.8％まで上昇した
後、23年末には+3％台まで鈍化したものの、24
年以降はプラス圏で推移し、25年後半には+6％
台まで加速した（図表6）。その後は伸びが鈍化
したものの、26年9月には＋4.4％と前月の＋
3.8％から小幅に加速した。 
電力料金の上昇には、ＡＩ関連投資の拡大に
伴うデータセンターの電力需要増加が影響して
いる可能性がある。データセンターは大量の電
力を安定的に消費するため、需要の急増に対し
て発電設備や送配電網の整備が追いつかない場
合には、電力需給の逼迫や設備投資費用の増加
を通じて電力料金の上昇圧力となる。また、電力インフラの整備には長い期間を要することから、
こうした影響が長期化する可能性もある。 
もっとも、電力料金は天然ガスなどの燃料価格や送配電設備の更新費用、地域ごとの料金規制な
ど複数の要因によって決定される。このため、足元の電力料金上昇のうち、ＡＩ関連投資による押
し上げ効果がどの程度を占めるかを特定することは難しい。今後もＡＩ関連投資の拡大が見込まれ
る中、電力需要の増加と供給能力の拡充の動向が、電力料金を通じたインフレ圧力を左右する要因
として注目される。

米国の電力料金上昇率（％）
（注）消費者物価指数（CPI）の電力料金。前年同月比
（資料）米労働統計局（BLS）よりニッセイ基礎研究所作成

### Page 5

（インフレ見通し）26年はエネルギー高で加速、27年は鈍化へ 
こうした中、中長期のインフレ期待が比較的安定していることも踏まえ、今後のインフレ率は、
26年中はエネルギー価格の上昇によって高止まりするものの、27年には関税による物価押し上げ
効果の剥落などから鈍化すると予想する。 
関税については、25年春以降の関税引き上げに伴う価格転嫁が一巡するほか、実効関税率がピー
ク時から低下していることもあって、今後の物価押し上げ効果は減衰するとみられる。一方、エネ
ルギー価格は中東情勢の緊迫化を背景に当面高止まりする可能性があるものの、27年には原油価格
の上昇一服や前年のベース効果の剥落から、 総合ＣＰＩへの押し上げ効果が縮小すると見込まれる。
また、ＡＩ関連投資の拡大に伴う電力需要の増加は、引き続き電力料金の上昇圧力となる可能性が
あるものの、ＣＰＩ全体への影響は限定的とみられる。 
当研究所では、総合ＣＰＩ上昇率（前年比）が25年の+2.6％から26年には+3.4％へ加速する一
方、27年には+2.5％へ鈍化すると予想する。もっとも、27年もＣＰＩ上昇率は2％台半ばで推移す
る見通しであり、インフレ圧力の完全な沈静化にはなお時間を要するとみられる。

（今後の注目点）エネルギー価格や追加関税などが上振れリスク 
今後のインフレ動向を巡っては、基本シナリオに対する上振れリスクとして、エネルギー価格の
一段の上昇や追加関税措置などが挙げられる。
